I don't really want to get into all of it right now, but... after all these months, I can see plain as day he's been trying to take over my place. Push me out. \n\nThat's what's going on.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your wife's relative moving your tools and ruining your fishing plans—do you ever wonder if there might be another explanation, or are you completely certain that he's doing this?\n\nPatient: No. I'm certain. \n\nLook... I know where I leave my things. I work with my hands for a living, I don't just misplace my gear. Like I said, they don't just grow legs and walk away. \n\nThere isn't another explanation. I know he's doing it. I wouldn't be sitting here if I wasn't sure.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing that your wife's relative has been doing these things to you—weeks, months, or longer?\n\nPatient: Months. \n\n...About nine months, I guess. Pretty much ever since he moved into our house.\n\nDoctor: During these nine months, have you ever heard voices, seen things, or experienced anything with your senses that others couldn't perceive—or has it mainly been about noticing what he's done to your belongings and plans?\n\nPatient: No. I don't hear voices or anything like that. \n\n...It's just my things being messed with. Like I said. That's it.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust—like your wife—told you that she didn't think your relative was moving your things, how would you respond to that?\n\nPatient: She does say that. Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it... I guess that's why I'm here. \n\nBut I know he came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves. My fishing plans don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. \n\n...After all these months, I can see it plain as day. He’s been trying to take over my place. Push me out. So... it doesn't really matter what she thinks. I know what's happening.\n\nDoctor: If I told you that I've reviewed everything you've described, and I couldn't find any evidence that your wife's relative is actually moving your tools or sabotaging your plans, would that change how you think about what's happening—or would you still be convinced he's doing it?\n\nPatient: No. It wouldn't change anything. \n\nLike I just said... I'm only sitting in this chair because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. But you not finding evidence doesn't mean it isn't happening. \n\nMy tools don’t move themselves. My fishing plans don't just get ruined every single weekend by accident. \n\n...I see it plain as day. He’s trying to take over my place and push me out. So... no. Whatever you think you reviewed, I'm still convinced. Because I know what he's doing.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned your wife's relative is trying to take over your place and push you out—can you walk me through how you think he's planning to do that? What's his overall strategy or goal?\n\nPatient: I don't know his exact plans. I just know what he's doing. \n\n...He messes with my stuff. He ruins my weekends. It's just... a way to break me down, I guess. Make me look crazy so my wife kicks me out. \n\nI haven't sat down and asked him his strategy. I just know he wants me gone. That's all there is to it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned he wants to break you down and make you look crazy so your wife kicks you out—but why do you think he wants you gone in the first place? What's his reason for wanting you out of the house?\n\nPatient: I don't know. ...You'd have to ask him. \n\nI just know he wants the place. I don't... I don't sit around trying to figure out why he is the way he is, or what his big reasons are. \n\nHe just wants me gone. That's all I know.\n\nDoctor: When your fishing plans get ruined on the weekend, how do you know for certain that your wife's relative did it—what specifically tells you it was him and not just bad luck or coincidence?\n\nPatient: ...I just know. It's not coincidence. You don't just... have bad luck every single time. \n\nI don't have a camera set up to catch him, if that's what you mean. It's just obvious. ...I don't really know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned he wants to break you down and make you look crazy so your wife will kick you out—and that he wants to take over your place. Do you think there are other things he might be doing to you beyond moving your tools and ruining your fishing plans? Or is it mainly just those two things?\n\nPatient: ...Mainly just what I told you. \n\nI don't know if he's doing other things. I haven't... I don't sit around keeping a list of every little thing. It's just the tools and my weekends. \n\nThat's enough, isn't it? I haven't really noticed anything else.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
...No. It's not impossible. 

I don't know why you'd ask that. He just... he walks into the garage and moves my tools. Anyone with hands can do that.
